# Application implementation task state

This checklist is the live PristinePage application task-state authority, not the historical documentation receipt. The operator approved local implementation under [P-01](OPERATIONAL_PLAN.md) on 2026-11-14. All acceptance checkboxes remain unimplemented/unverified at setup. Use [IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md](IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md) for exact files, interfaces, test steps and commands. Claim metadata belongs here; cross-file requirements/test links belong in [REQUIREMENTS_TRACEABILITY.md](REQUIREMENTS_TRACEABILITY.md).

### Library storage and backup — T-03 / LIBRARY-PORTABILITY-01

Coordinator on `feat/library-portability`, public input `01941bc`. User-approved [library portability contract](DATA_MODEL_AND_STORAGE.md#library-portability-amendment): new format, shared conflict planner and transfer protocol, verified temporary catalog, missing-object copies only, and explicit copy versus move semantics. Start with the pure `core/.../library` planner and its JVM tests. Whole-library round trip (private/default and selected roots), live Room cutover/rollback, background ownership, capacity, and the Android Settings/permission flow remain open. Use SimpleStorage only at the permission adapter boundary after exact dependency admission; do not upgrade the existing toolchain implicitly.

Planner checkpoint: independent source review found no Critical issues and three Important cases. Four focused host tests reproduced reused-ID/name mismatches (including cross-album IDs) and Greek final-sigma mismatch. All four passed after correction; the strict-offline run recorded 317 core and 15 fixture cases, no failures or skips, including 14 planner cases. Same-digest/different-length and fingerprint-only mismatches have direct controls. The planner consumes stable, bounded metadata; it does not prove byte equality, remap catalog rows or make retries durable. Next course check is after the missing-object transfer witness and before trusting it in a staged Room round trip, or within 30 active minutes. Keep APK, dependency acquisition, devices and other feature work outside this host-only block.

Immutable transfer checkpoint: 10 intended host failures against the empty implementation; final strict-offline `:core:test :test-fixtures:test :data:testDebugUnitTest :image-android:testDebugUnitTest :app:testDebugUnitTest` recorded 1,711 JVM cases, no failures or skips, including 11 transfer tests. The first combined run stopped because the new worktree lacked the standard OCR stage; the existing exact approved 124-pack stage was verified in its original checkout and copied to the ignored worktree path without acquisition. The bounded stream loop is reused from album/text export. The helper validates closed bytes, compares content before reuse, preflights only missing payloads plus explicitly budgeted catalog/journal space, and reuses a completed object after a later object fails. It gives no catalog publication receipt, no deletion authority, and no property about the durability of an arbitrary provider.

Transfer review found no Critical issue. Before using this helper in a published catalog or move, cover a write/close/promotion failure with the real adapter, and require known absence, exclusive nonaliased roots, preserved source snapshot and exact destination generation through final reopen. Admit byte limits, aggregate verification work and catalog/reference counts before opening caller-selected files; reserve actual capacity including any backend staging overhead. Short-read asymmetry and explicit visibility-error coverage remain open. Course check: the next main-lane deliverable is a private export → reopened candidate/catalog slice using existing Room validation, not UI alone. The Android permission gate now targets [SimpleStorage 3.1.0](OPERATIONAL_PLAN.md#library-storage-and-simplestorage); the supported SDK/AGP migration is distinct from host data work. Recheck at the first durable-catalog test outcome or within 30 active minutes.

Generation checkpoint: independent marker review confirmed the canonical v1 vector and requested exact extent/malformed coverage; independent publication review found no Critical and two Important issues. Both reproduced at JUnit: a visible marker was reported successful during failed durability, and the reread could substitute different catalog bytes. The publisher now hands the synchronous verifier one exact private copy, checks the actual destination again, confirms payload durability before the completion marker, and requires positive marker durability on replay as well as first publication. The app-owned local adapter publishes closed stages without overwriting using an exclusive same-filesystem link, refuses unsupported directory durability, and retains failed stages separately. Four actual host-filesystem witnesses exercise partial copy, uncertain sync, overwrite refusal and unsupported durability. The final strict-offline data invocation passed 855 JVM cases, including 18 generation/publication/local-file cases, with no failures or skips. These are injected sync and catalog-verifier tests, not a real Android Room, provider, power-loss, activation or cleanup witness. Next: bind read-only validation of a closed populated Room catalog, then exact row and OCR projection into the export → fresh import journey.

Catalog reader checkpoint: the missing reader and header checks reproduced at Kotlin compilation. Independent read-only review confirmed Android's default corruption handler could delete a corrupt verification copy and a migrated live Room database is not a valid raw-DDL oracle for freshly written portable catalogs. Use a non-deleting error handler, a fresh in-memory Room schema reference, a bounded SQLite header with exact physical page count and no sidecars, and read-only schema/identity/quick/FK checks. The next writer must project validated records into a fresh Room database and rebuild indexes, not install untrusted SQLite pages directly; `quick_check` is not a full index or data check. Strict offline 858 data JVM cases and 196 host SQLite checks passed, and six Android fixtures compiled, including corrupt-file preservation; Android execution remains open. An independent normalizer constraint also applies: original bytes alone do not prove cross-build source reproducibility, since the existing fingerprint includes the Android build. The next core witness remains a coherent private export → fresh import with ordered scans, edits, and searchable OCR. Do not promote the adapter, marker, and read-only verifier into user-facing completion.

Scan names checkpoint: the absent allocator reproduced at Kotlin compilation, followed by 322 core and 15 fixture JVM cases with no failures or skips. New ingest can preserve a bounded provider display name or derive an opaque page-ID label with an extension from the admitted media type; equivalent names are disambiguated under the same comparison as merge without splitting a Unicode surrogate pair. Labels never become storage paths. The allocator is not wired into ingest yet. Persist it with the page in the writer transaction, including deliberate duplicates and wizard append. Do not infer historical provider names from `original.bin` or other paths.

Scan-name storage checkpoint: coordinator owns the additive Room v7→v8 migration and exported schema. The missing migration reproduced in four actual-SQL host checks; the final 202-case suite includes fresh/migrated DDL parity, exact retained v7 pages and two retry-consent rows, album-scoped uniqueness, cross-album FK refusal, and soft-removal/hard-purge behavior. Independent review found no blocking production issue and noted two fixture gaps addressed by the final host cases. Strict offline recorded 1,737 JVM cases without failures or skips and compiled the updated v1–v7 populated Room fixture; no emulator execution. No old schema, previous scan, private permission, or filename is overwritten or synthesized by this migration. The separate named-ingest fixture deliberately reproduces the absent provider name port and is not yet a passing Android case. The next step is to persist the exact name under the ingest/page transaction, then project the named saved catalog and its OCR into a fresh export.

Named-ingest review on input `04361b7`: the first implementation could acknowledge success after a changed terminal job and could replay a newly allocated fallback without its name. Both reproduced on the host; v3 now requires the allocated name even without a provider hint, and readback checks the terminal job and exact committed receipt before acknowledging. The real-SQL collision test reproduced the missing indexed lookup. Replace the retained-name list cap with exact album/key seeks and switch to an opaque-ID label when the preferred suffix range is full. Late duplicate/append writes are covered by Android trigger fixtures, not counted as runtime passes. The optional provider query has a bounded wait and one in-flight slot; it still cannot guarantee that provider I/O stops or bound a provider's internal allocations. Existing broad private ingest journal/lease reads are not untrusted backup parsing and remain a separate hardening concern. The round trip remains the main delivery gate.

Named-ingest host evidence: focused JUnit recorded two intended failing assertions (missing v3 no-hint support and an incorrectly acknowledged terminal job). The later missing optional lookup and bounded allocator reproduced at compilation; the compatibility checks exposed a stale future-version fixture and an unbound seek in the host recovery harness before the final run. Strict offline recorded 1,743 JVM cases with zero failures or skips across the five modules, and compiled data/app Android tests. The focused real-SQL host suite includes the album/key index witness. Current-build Room replay, trigger rollback and provider behavior still require Android execution; there was no signing, device action, dependency admission, push or integration.

Next course checkpoint, input `86eccd4`: obtain the real local source → closed catalog → fresh restore witness, retaining album/page order, original length/digest and bytes, accepted recipe and history, and searchable saved OCR. Start with the mandatory saved-OCR conversion and the unfinished-operation refusal in the source snapshot. Scope: data library staging, narrowly reused OCR metadata and verification ports, synthetic host/Android fixtures and this ledger; leave package configuration, active-root switching and deletion out until the round trip exists. The missing OCR projection reproduced at Android test compilation and the scalar admission checks failed before implementation. Reassess after the reviewed saved-OCR result and before starting staged export, or within 30 active minutes. An authenticated exact source snapshot and destination reopen are still required; no APK or LDPlayer scope is implied.

Saved catalog checkpoint: capture the actual completed OCR producer independently of a discarded rerun's payload, reject dangling or contradictory result provenance, and rebuild the index from verbatim text. Refuse an unfinished draft or import; a draft marked committed needs bounded, decodable promotion evidence, including for append. The stage carries accepted edits, history, logical filenames, source/day metadata, and retained removed pages; their bytes are not deleted to get a successful export. Rebuild PAGE and RECIPE_HISTORY ownership in the fresh catalog and keep its original file references separate from payload verification. The review also required detached recipe bytes and an empty-stage check that refuses standalone journal rows. Masked/unsupported recipe history still refuses; preserving executable Undo commands through import, reference remapping, and authentic native integration remain open. The stage is not the active library and is not by itself a backup.

Independent review covered the saved-OCR and catalog-write boundaries. The positive test now supplies a corrupt excluded rerun and a decoy source index token; other compiled Room fixtures cover missing or misnumbered text, a malformed committed draft, retained removal, mutable recipe handoff and a residual private journal. Host SQLite reproduced the false committed-draft acceptance and missing exhaustive stage check; the corrected storage suite passed 210 cases. Strict-offline affected JVM suites and Android test compilation passed. The Room fixtures have not run; do not claim that index rebuilding, migrations, on-disk reopen, full Undo preservation or cross-device render compatibility passed on Android.

Local folder export review: require a previously absent canonical app-private target claimed by exclusive directory creation, not a general picker URI or an existing empty path. Check SQLite page budget before staging saved rows, and preflight aggregate unique original bytes plus metadata before opening any original. Before publishing, read the closed catalog, admit bounded original references and compare them to the staged intent; after payload transfer, reopen the authenticated destination catalog and verify its objects. Retire only the new attempt's redundant scratch catalog after verified success; stop creating retained private stages after the bounded limit rather than delete uncertain evidence. The source folder is never retired here. Discovery of a local backup tip rejects ambiguous chains and does not authorize activation. The next meaningful checkpoint is a bounded read-only open of an authenticated backup followed by exact object restore into a fresh library; folder grants, live merges, settings, executable Undo/masks and LDPlayer evidence remain open.

The independent local export review prompted the exclusive-target, stage-growth, capacity and scratch-retention corrections above. Strict-offline verification recorded 1,748 JVM cases without failures or skips, including the generation-chain and local directory discovery cases, and compiled the Android data fixtures; 212 host storage checks passed. The Android witnesses for ordered scan bindings, damaged originals and refusing a preexisting folder did not run. No filesystem durability or Room reopen on Android is asserted from compilation.

`LIBRARY-PORTABILITY-01` continuation from `aba27c0`: authenticate the single selected local tip into independent private scratch, then read the schema and original locators and verify the actual byte objects without writing in the backup. A successful read is not a full catalog admission or import authority. The next active witness is a closed private backup → fresh isolated Room catalog plus originals → exact page/history/metadata and per-page saved OCR reopen. Validate all projected rows and their ownership before copying payloads; rebuild derived references and FTS and do not project private journals. Scope is library data code and conformance fixtures only; no selected provider, active library, cleanup of previous libraries, signing or emulator changes. Recheck after the first independently reviewed reader outcome and the first restore result, or within 30 active minutes.

Read checkpoint: the lower-level scratch-overlap refusal and linked-object error classification each reproduced one focused JVM failure before correction. Independent review confirmed exact closed-copy proof and source nonmutation under stable cooperative ownership. Normalize only the path/iteration I/O refusals; a reread of the catalog and tip does not create a concurrency fence. The final data JVM suite passed all 875 cases, including eight backup-reader cases, without failures or skips. The Android reader fixture compiled before the final error-normalization correction; it did not run. This evidence covers a locator read, not projection of untrusted metadata or restoration.

Fresh restore checkpoint, input `90252b3`: the missing Android restore facade reproduced at test compilation. The candidate separates closed catalog parsing from the writable catalog and projects only its supported saved rows, with derived pins and FTS rebuilt. The fresh target is never the active library. The synthetic on-disk Room witness checks reordered names, original bytes, capture-day metadata, accepted rotation and history, and exact saved OCR with its recorded producer, without running recognition. It uses the controlled ingest inspector rather than the application's actual image decoder. The witness compiled but has not run; real Android round trip and cross-device source compatibility remain open. Reassess at the independent restore review before applying any merge plan to a destination.

Restore review corrections: host SQLite reproduced both a leading-NUL conversion fingerprint the editor would refuse and a wrong index key that `quick_check` accepted. Require the ordinary source guard's character bound and full SQLite index integrity before projection. The closing capacity check now reserves only remaining object/filesystem costs, instead of subtracting the already written stage again. The completion contract now identifies the exact rebuilt catalog and publishes an independent receipt after reopening it; the old return value identified only the input. A visible artifact after failed sync remains uncertain and is not eligible for automatic adoption. Durable replay of an interrupted restore requires a separate recovery reader, and this slice never changes the active root.

The reviewed offline invocation recorded 1,756 JVM cases without failures or skips and compiled the data Android tests. The host storage suite passed 219 cases, including the exact SQLite index and editor-admission regressions; the isolated library subset passed all 25. The real restore, closed receipt and unchanged-source assertions are compiled but unrun on Android. No schema, dependency version, production app behavior, signing or device state changed.

Next runtime gate: use the pinned toolchain to sign only the exact, independently reviewed `:data` self-target instrumentation candidate with the existing development identity, and run the named catalog, export and restore witnesses on one exclusively owned released LDPlayer guest after signing/device scope is recorded. Use only synthetic app-private input and fresh target folders. Do not replace any unrelated app/test package, reset data, or query a physical phone. The first restore witness and true corruption refusal are independent of the proposed SimpleStorage toolchain migration. Do not expose a selected-folder restore or claim the user journey before resolving this native gate.

The [app-private Android restore witness](../../validation/storage/library-restore-runtime.md) subsequently passed all 18 exact named tests on LDPlayer API 28. Preserve the distinction between real closed Room/file verification and the controlled ingest and OCR fixtures. Both borrowed guests' existing packages were preserved; the one fresh guest is stopped and released, with its disk retained. No active or selected library was changed. The next core slice is a merge dry run that composes a fresh candidate catalog from two admitted snapshots without writing either library or copying originals. Reuse the shared conflict planner, keep destination-only pages and history, remap incoming IDs and references, and reject a candidate that exceeds catalog limits before payload writes. Recheck at the first candidate readback/review or within 30 active minutes. Exact-byte comparison, durable receipt/replay, active-root fencing, masks and executable Undo remain separate gates.

Merge dry-run rulings: preserve destination slot identity and order; append incoming revisions above all retained local revisions with parent pointers remapped. For identical accepted recipe bytes and absent incoming OCR, keep the exact local producing revision and job active instead of inventing a new input for that job; retain the imported history. If the incoming recipe differs and has no OCR, separate the album rather than silently lose the only saved text or bind it to a new recipe. Conflicting retained-page state or capture metadata likewise keeps the incoming album intact. The initial format can represent only one current saved OCR result per slot; incoming saved text replaces the local current result only when the accepted-merge policy authorizes that precedence. These rules avoid hidden scan or metadata loss at the cost of separate albums in unresolved cases.

The independent dry-run review found no Critical and three Important issues. Reproduced local OCR provenance renumbering, shared-original capture-day disagreement within/across albums, and differing album order between candidate and reopen. The corrected candidate preserves equivalent local OCR without rewriting its intent hash, separates all incoming albums affected by a shared-original metadata conflict, and follows closed-reader album ID order. Aggregate budget checks include empty albums; the dimension fixture now has internally valid source/encoded shapes. Strict-offline verification recorded 1,765 JVM cases, including nine merge cases, with zero failures or skips. The Android fixture that writes and reopens a fresh merged SQLite catalog compiled but did not run on this candidate. No files were copied between libraries, published, activated, or removed. Next: connect the staged catalog to both verified original inventories, compare matches byte-for-byte, and prove read-only preflight before a local copy writer may act. Keep SimpleStorage and active-folder switching out of that slice.

Local merge preflight builds and reopens a private candidate SQLite file from two admitted portable generations, then checks exact-byte coverage and estimates only missing payloads plus catalog/headroom. Neither input library changes. A missing object from the existing destination is an integrity failure, not an implicit repair from the backup. Readback rechecks both input generations and returns an observation, not a reservation, activation token or lock. Independent review found an Important gap in derived OCR readback: keep exact incoming raw text independent of index tokens, rebuild into fresh tables, explicitly check the app-built FTS index on its owning write connection, and verify the reopened staged canonical indexed text. Keep an altered indexed-text/native fixture and the actual SQL posting-mismatch witness. All 888 data JVM cases (four new real-filesystem payload checks) and 230 host storage checks passed; both native witness methods compiled. Broader negative filesystem staging coverage was deferred; return to it at the first write slice. The next runtime slot is the same dedicated guest, for the exact checked data successor only.

First copy-writer checkpoint: take a separately verified source and destination generation under cooperative ownership, bind their exact identities and the closed candidate to one private durable operation intent, then publish only missing immutable objects. Reopen the actual next destination generation before reporting it committed; keep the predecessor and leave the source untouched. If interrupted, only the same persisted intent may resume; an already visible marker requires full revalidation and durable readback, never another merge. Refuse changed or aliased roots, an exhausted generation chain, unexpected ownership or an uncertain/invalid intent before any additional writes. Obtain interruption/replay and blocked-cleanup evidence before composing active-root or move behavior. The user-facing selected-folder and SimpleStorage gate remain separate.

The first merge-preflight Android candidate passed 19/20 named tests: all 18 previous restore/catalog/OCR checks plus read-only preflight against two exported folders. The negative staged OCR fixture reached a standalone-header refusal after its test-only writable reopen, not the intended OCR guard; the preliminary successful candidate readback ran. Keep the original failed transcript. Explicitly set and check rollback-journal mode on the test-only connection before altering indexed text. No production guard changes. Verify the corrected data fixture against the exact installed predecessor and preserve both artifact receipts.

The rollback-journal fixture passed that boundary but surfaced a second test-only assumption: precomposed Latin text was already canonical, so the real writer stored it solely in `indexedText`. The subsequent corruption replaced the only stored copy by design. Keep that 19/20 transcript as well. Use decomposed `cafe\u0301` to force the real writer to store both exact verbatim text and the distinct NFC search projection. Assert both actual columns before mutation and require the precise staged-row refusal after changing only the indexed copy. No production code or guard changes in this fixture correction.

The [corrected merge dry-run Android follow-up](../../validation/storage/library-restore-runtime.md#merge-dry-run-follow-up) passed 20/20 actual API 28 cases, including both merged-catalog cases and the exact closed-stage OCR refusal. The one dedicated guest is stopped and released. With the read-only path witnessed, the next core step is the verified write and its exact durable intent/replay; do not treat a preview, staged file or visible marker as success authority.

Copy intent and private journal checkpoint: the bounded canonical record binds both opaque root keys, both source/baseline markers, and the exact next catalog. Resume requires the installed schema and complete marker identity, distinguishes writing against the unchanged baseline from verifying an already published exact child, and refuses locally known generation reuse. Persist only with an already admitted, closed private stage; force the catalog and its directory chain through a caller-supplied known-durable ancestor before intent publication, and recheck the receipt and stage after uncertain acknowledgment. The reviews identified unsupported schema/extent and reproduced ancestor-ID reuse and a failed higher directory barrier; ten focused cases are included in 898 passing data JVM tests, and Android test sources compile. No journal consumer yet fences the entire destination chain or writes into either library. Next: use the intent in a narrowly scoped local copy writer; verify exact bytes, destination readback and idempotent recovery before attempting activation.

The app-private copy publication kernel now consumes that exact intent under caller-owned stable roots. It checks appendability against the bounded marker inventory, copies only missing immutable originals and reopens authenticated actual destination bytes before the completion marker; it neither switches roots nor deletes the predecessor. Independent review reproduced acceptance of a changed predecessor marker on replay and skipping a non-directory object namespace. Require both exact marker records and a positive object-directory barrier for a nonempty catalog. A visible child is verification-only, not permission to rebuild missing bytes; its private verification copy does not require destination free space. Strict-offline `:data:testDebugUnitTest :data:compileDebugAndroidTestKotlin` recorded 909 JVM tests with zero failures or skips, including nine real-filesystem copy/replay cases with synthetic sync and catalog callbacks. No full disk, forced power loss, native Room copy writer, provider, active root, or cleanup claim follows. Next: prove real Room stage → copy → fresh reopen on a synthetic app-private pair, with an unchanged source and replay of the exact staged catalog, before composing active-root behavior. Recheck at that native witness or within 30 active minutes.

Detached Room copy boundary: the preview captures both physical root identities and exact generations. A single app-owned, cooperatively locked journal namespace survives across facade instances. Resume with the operation ID only if that journal already binds both requested roots and the original generations; rebuild the expected projection for comparison, not a new staged catalog. Check chain capacity and vacant ID before a retained stage is created. The Android witness compiles and must reject a byte-identical substitute root, interrupt at the copied object's directory barrier, refuse the same ID against a different root, and reopen the exact child with original bytes, retained predecessor, edit history and searchable verbatim OCR. The strict-offline data suite recorded 911 passing JVM tests, including exact retained-generation admission and refusal of a changed ancestor marker. Retained scratch is bounded to four operations in this internal slice; completion acknowledgement and owned maintenance are required before offering an ongoing production workflow. Selected folders, active-root workers, cleanup and actual process-kill/power-loss remain separate gates.

The [journaled Room copy witness](../../validation/storage/library-restore-runtime.md#journaled-room-copy-follow-up) passed 21/21 API 28 cases on the exact data-only successor. The injected post-link failure left the predecessor selected; after exact-intent resume, the child reopened with both exact originals and searchable saved OCR, and another resume wrote neither library. The next dependency for activation is a durable active-library identity and generation fence. OCR delivery currently transports page and job IDs only; replacing the current in-memory graph alone would let stale deliveries describe the new library. Keep the previous graph usable until a new graph and selection can be verified, and require selected-but-unavailable storage to reconnect rather than opening the default. Run the first identity/fence checks before any product wiring or old-library cleanup.

The process-local fence uses the persistent library UUID plus a monotonically increasing activation number; reopening the same library after a switch does not reuse its previous activation. While a graph is leased, switching is refused; while a switch is writing, no graph is leased. Definite refusal keeps the old graph, uncertain publication keeps both graphs fenced until an exact durable selection is verified, and a revoked selected folder admits only that same selection after reconnect. The missing fence reproduced at Kotlin compilation; all four targeted JVM cases passed in the 915-case data suite. No production worker or graph uses it yet. Next: persist a private activation pointer with strict absence/corruption handling and a verified atomic replacement, then bind actual foreground and OCR operation lifetimes to the same fence.

The private selection record distinguishes positive absence from malformed or inaccessible storage, binds the library UUID, activation number, and (for a selected folder) the opaque grant and admitted catalog generation IDs. It forces a closed checksummed stage, atomically replaces the pointer without fallback, and requires a successful directory barrier and reread before acknowledgment. Independent review reproduced seven failures across missed concurrent revocation, mistaken refusal on predecessor drift, canonical-but-corrupt activation, and unsupported sync discovered after replacement. All seven pass after correction, in 924 passing data JVM tests; the private-filesystem Android witness compiles and is not yet run. The checksum detects accidental corruption, not replay of a whole old file. The caller still owns first-use proof, exclusivity, an already durable settings directory, and exact reopened storage/graph validation. A published pointer may select an unavailable folder; no pointer return or process fence outcome grants retirement authority.

[Private pointer runtime evidence](../../validation/storage/library-restore-runtime.md#private-activation-pointer-follow-up) passed 22/22 API 28 cases. This exercises atomic replacement and failed-acknowledgment recovery on app-private Android storage, not actual folder grants or an app cutover. Next: bind the active-root grant and exact admitted generation to a verified storage/reopen capability, then retain it through actual repository, editor and OCR operations and ensure background wakes carry the issuing activation, not merely page/job IDs.

Graph-lease checkpoint: pair the private fence with an owned, verified graph capability. Review required allocation-site handoff for cancellation-safe cleanup, separation of graph preparation from the publication boundary, and a structured lease that must return detached results. A candidate revoked before publication is discarded; a failure after publication starts fences both identities until exact reconciliation. Failed graph teardown is tracked independently and does not roll back a durable switch. Seven targeted cases passed in 931 data JVM tests; Android test sources compile. The graph type is synthetic here. The next decisive witness must use reopened catalog records and exact provider/root identity without moving originals for Open; the ordinary application still cannot switch libraries. No change to toolchain or app dependencies.

Selected-folder boundary remains gated: SimpleStorage identity/grant handling cannot itself prove exclusive file creation, durable visibility, directory sync, or safe deletion on a provider. Keep a read-only folder open and verified write capability separate; never infer the latter by converting a `content:` URI to a raw path. The default/private journal and graph evidence do not license enabling arbitrary SAF destinations. Before offering move or import into a selected folder, exercise an approved local provider with the exact supported toolchain and test permission revocation and durability refusal.

Detached Open checkpoint: bind the app-private folder's physical identity and marker at proposal, reopen the confirmed exact generation through a bounded SQLite verification copy, and read original bytes in place without materializing a second scan tree. Only the verifier constructs the admitted view; it checks the current root, catalog and tip before and after a read. This assumes stable cooperative ownership and is not a lock. The single Android fixture compiles and checks byte-identical root substitution, same-length original corruption and a published successor; 931 data JVM cases remain green. Large supported libraries may require substantial full-inventory hashing, so keep admission cancellable and off interactive reads. The next native run must exercise this exact detached open; no live Room graph, external grant or published catalog is created here.

[Detached Open runtime evidence](../../validation/storage/library-restore-runtime.md#detached-library-open-follow-up) passed 23/23 API 28 cases. Existing originals remained in place; the verified view refused an exact-byte replacement directory and a changed tip. Work proceeds on authenticating newly decoded pixels against the recorded conversion identity; this test did not use a real codec or cross-device transfer. The existing toolchain approval remains pending for the selected-folder picker and grants.

Historical pixel-binding checkpoint: for one admitted source record and stable verified original, the decoder can compare actual normalized RGB under the stored fingerprint while allowing a different current build with the same known conversion policy. Keep byte extent/digest, encoded and normalized shape, EXIF, profile and policy checks independent; this does not rewrite stored provenance or enable ordinary render or export replay. The strict-offline image suite passed 283 JVM cases. The synthetic on-device codec witness compiles, including a same-length alternate PNG with the same normalized pixels that must fail the original's byte binding. Its image-module APK has not been signed or installed: the current portability guest is scoped to data-only successors. A synthesized historical fingerprint does not prove two-build equivalence.

Start-empty checkpoint: claim only the same positively empty detached local directory seen at confirmation, stage and reopen a fresh schema with zero originals, and ask for no source root or cleanup capability. The review narrowed implicit durability to the known-durable app files ancestor and put all verification spill files inside one of four admitted attempt directories. A write or cancellation after possible marker visibility requires reconciliation rather than reinitialization; a read-only open is not acknowledgment. Android fixtures cover intervening unrelated content and an injected post-marker directory failure. The next check is the privately scoped runtime witness before considering Settings or activation.

[Start-empty runtime evidence](../../validation/storage/library-restore-runtime.md#start-empty-follow-up) passed 25/25 data-only API 28 cases; the dedicated guest is stopped and released. The next user-value checkpoint is the actual selected-folder grant and durable app cutover. The SimpleStorage toolchain scope is still awaiting approval; do not install a dependency, swap the active app package, or expose Settings as working while that gate is open. Portable Undo and masks, clean acknowledgment of an initially visible marker after process death, active-root worker binding, and move retirement remain open.

Cross-module checkpoint: strict-offline `:core:test :test-fixtures:test :data:testDebugUnitTest :image-android:testDebugUnitTest :app:testDebugUnitTest` recorded 1,813 JVM cases with zero failures, errors or skips (323 core, 15 fixtures, 931 data, 283 image, 261 app); 230 host storage checks passed. The app package and its ordinary behavior were not exercised on the data-only guest. For the next selected-folder iteration, admit the approved exact toolchain first, then prove one actual local grant → validated catalog → retained active graph before adding writable operations. If that gate remains closed, the independent work queue is portable Undo, checked mask encoding/objects, and verified cleanup refusal; none is a substitute for an actual granted-folder journey.

Undo codec checkpoint: the ordinary recipe and membership Undo writers share the existing byte formats with a bounded canonical remapper. The preflight counts actual nesting outside quoted strings; the focused test reproduced a quote/bracket sequence defeating the original depth guard. All five focused tests passed in the 936-case data JVM suite; Android test sources compiled. Exact existing ordered recipe Undo bytes still execute through the ordinary repository. IDs, source bindings, retained revisions and uniqueness are checked during remap, but a remapped byte record does not authorize insertion or execution. Next: admit both halves as one entry, verify exact album/page/revision membership and retention against the actual projected catalog, and rebuild source/mask pins without turning stale or expired entries into executable ones. The portable catalog still refuses Undo rows pending that witness.

`LIBRARY-PORTABILITY-01-TOOLCHAIN` on `feat/library-portability`: the [scoped toolchain approval](OPERATIONAL_PLAN.md#library-storage-and-simplestorage) is now recorded. Before connecting the actual picker or publishing any selected-folder write, prove API 37.0 compilation on Gradle 9.3.1 and AGP 9.1.1 with SimpleStorage 3.1.0, unchanged app SDK limits, and both merged manifests free of broad storage grants. Scope is build inputs, affected locks, merged-manifest checks and this ledger; no picker or live graph behavior changes, signing, device, or publication. Coordinator owns the sole build slot; recheck at the first strict dependency-resolution result or within 30 active minutes. Keep the grant → authenticated catalog → durable graph → reconnect journey as the next core witness; do not substitute successful build output for it.

Picker boundary finding: the upstream [SimpleStorage 3.1.0 folder contract](https://github.com/anggrayudi/SimpleStorage/blob/b3fce1d2479d57fe738e7bb82cc03ef856cd474f/storage/src/main/java/com/anggrayudi/storage/contract/SimpleStorageResultContracts.kt) checks broad storage permission on API 26–28 and can start asynchronous cleanup of persisted child grants. Do not use that contract or the suspend facade that delegates to it. Keep Android's exact tree result and persisted modes under app control, use SimpleStorage only to resolve an already admitted tree-backed folder, and reject any conversion to `file:` or loss of the exact original tree identity. Test tentative selection, missing/read-only grants, unrelated-grant preservation, and revoked/unmounted reconnect before wiring the picker to the active graph.

Toolchain verification in progress: the installed API 37.0 revision 2 and existing Build Tools 36.0.0 are present. The Gradle 9.3.1 distribution and regenerated wrapper JAR match the publisher's SHA-256; the regenerated wrapper reports 9.3.1 in the isolated cache. The strict AGP resolution stopped at missing verification metadata, first for the plugin marker and then for 94 classpath artifacts. Verify their publisher checksums before admitting the plugin, then obtain a real red/green result from both merged manifests and replay affected tests offline. Neither the configuration refusal nor the verified wrapper is evidence that the application builds.

Toolchain course check: verified AGP configuration offline, then reproduced both legacy permissions in the actual pre-fix debug and release manifests. The permission removals and regression assertions are in place. Remaining work in this support slice is the late AGP lint/compiler and runtime verification entries, refreshed locks and full strict-offline replay; do not widen the graph or change app behavior to avoid that check. The first admission still uses Windows and installed SDK/JBR only, with no signing or guest mutation. Next checkpoint is the verified final dependency graph and first exact offline replay, or within the next 30 active minutes.

Next core boundary: keep selected-tree reads separate from `LocalLibraryDirectory`'s exclusive-path, write and directory-sync assumptions. The chosen tree must resolve as the same Android document-tree identity using SimpleStorage's narrow tree wrapper, while provider lookup errors and lost grants remain distinct from positive absence. Reuse the existing bounded marker/catalog/byte verification with a read-only port; a transient, verified private spool for one decoder input is not authority to duplicate the library or to commit an external write. Recheck the actual provider and acceptance with an authorized real local folder before exposing a live cutover.

Toolchain support milestone passed: the [strict-offline locked replay and artifact policy receipt](../../validation/bootstrap/library-toolchain-upgrade.md) records 1,818 JVM cases, 230 host storage checks, Android test compilation, zero lint errors and the unsigned artifact inspection. API 26/36 and the previous standalone Coroutines graph remain unchanged; the app uses the approved SimpleStorage closure and both merged manifests refuse broad storage. The independent review corrections are recorded. No APK was signed or installed. The selected-folder grant, active graph, and product backup/move tests are still open.

`LIBRARY-PORTABILITY-01-READ-PORT` on `835cfa3`: separate the shared backup verifier from the concrete file-backed directory without widening its authority. Scope: data library read source, detached generation inventory, existing local-path overlap guard and JVM regressions. Verify exact catalog/selected-generation readback and unique originals from a read-only stream source, refused conflicting extents before any original opens, and failure rather than success if the source disappears during the final check. Review the local write boundaries independently. The next check is the affected data JVM suite and Android test-source compilation, then the first exact selected-tree adapter; no provider or activation is supplied by this port alone.

The independent read-port review found one Important cancellation regression (the final catalog open could begin after cancellation during the last original's close), and one Minor contract gap (catalog open must distinguish positive absence from an inaccessible source). Retain the prior end-of-originals checkpoint before any final open and exercise both error paths without changing local append/restore ownership. The next provider adapter must reject ambiguous child identities and failed or partial enumeration, and should reuse one bounded object inventory rather than rescan the same provider directory for each original.

For the first actual grant witness, resolve the exact local `content:` tree through SimpleStorage without using its stock activity contract or path conversion. Check the returned tree, requested persisted mode, existing grant inventory and the reopened Android document identity separately. A rejected or cancelled selection leaves the current pointer, unrelated grants and files unchanged. This is read-only: route it through catalog admission first; do not expose selected-folder mutation until the separate durability and activation bindings exist. The retained data-only guest cannot exercise an ordinary app picker.

Read-port checks: the missing stream source interface reproduced at test compilation. The review's cancellation regression then reproduced as one real failing assertion (two catalog opens rather than one); the paired catalog absence/access error control passed. After restoring the barrier, the normal strict-offline retry passed 941 data JVM cases including 15 backup-read cases and compiled data Android tests. The first post-fix retry stopped during a Kotlin `kotlin-backups…` failure consistent with the earlier [INC-008](common-mistakes-and-frequently-used-commands.md#inc-008--kotlin-backup-cleanup-failure-and-powershell-property-splitting); no cache cleanup or toolchain change was required. The 230 host storage checks passed on this increment. No Android provider, activation or write path was exercised.

Selected-tree lookup slice: the data reader translates only the fixed `generations`, `catalogs` and `objects` names and authenticated marker/hash-derived child names. A provider supplies bounded complete directory snapshots and opens by opaque document ID; an incomplete list, duplicate managed name or reused document ID is not proof of absence. Read the object directory once per admission, preserve unrelated entries without opening them, and do not infer write capability from listing. Keep Android grant acquisition and mounted-root/identity proof in the next adapter.

Provider adapter acceptance must retain the original tree URI and the exact returned grant modes, use Android's descendant URI builder only for IDs enumerated from the agreed parent, and propagate no-cursor or loading/error state instead of reporting absence. Recheck the persisted permission at reads and after recreation. Synthetic JVM streams verify the catalog rules, not Android's authorization, mount behavior, actual Files selection or process-loss reconnect. Keep the prior app-private library selected until that real witness and the current-selection publication boundary are verified.

Tree lookup review found two Important gaps, both reproduced: an object ID could be reused under another parent, and cancellation at provider calls could be misclassified as absence or cause further I/O. Reject a conflicting parent/name/type binding across the whole attempt, budget retained identity text, check cancellation before and after list/read, and close an already opened marker without reading it when cancelled. The review's fresh-generation coverage note now has positive refusals for a byte-identical catalog with a new document ID and for a new tip during projection. Three intended review failures became green in the final strict-offline run of 950 data JVM cases (nine focused tree cases); 230 host storage checks passed and data Android test sources compiled. The host adapter still assumes a cooperating, already authorized backend and cannot establish Android grant, identity, write, or durability acceptance.

Next actionable gate: implement the Android document-tree backend and exact grant handoff, then demonstrate Files → exact persisted tree → verified catalog and reconnect on loss. The current released guest is data-only and cannot receive that app/test pair. A new test run needs an exact permitted artifact/guest combination and adequate live disk; final host check left 8.49 GiB (about 504 MiB above the 8 GiB floor), too little unreserved headroom for another app packaging and guest run. Preserve all retained artifacts and guests, and do not bypass the disk guard or perform cleanup without authorization. Current host witnesses are not a reason to enable Settings operations or selected-folder writes.

The operator [authorized integration after portability acceptance](OPERATIONAL_PLAN.md#library-portability-integration-after-acceptance): classic merge with a self-contained intention, separate SemVer commit, and guarded push of `main` only. It does not clear the open feature or device gates. The next live check measured 7.43 GiB free, below the host build floor; resume gated implementation/build verification once the supported workspace has enough space for the next bounded run above that floor.

`LIBRARY-PORTABILITY-01-ANDROID-TREE`: the operator freed disk and authorized [LDPlayer app runtime qualification](OPERATIONAL_PLAN.md#library-portability-app-runtime-qualification). The resumed host check measured 20.83 GiB free, not a reserved launch. The first slice is read-only: admit the exact system-returned tree URI and required persisted mode without touching unrelated grants; verify the selected directory, bounded complete child enumeration, catalog and originals through the existing reader; refuse unavailable/replaced roots instead of falling back. Use only the approved app target for an actual Files witness. Neither this scope nor catalog verification alone authorizes a live root switch or selected-folder writes.

First Android boundary candidate: system Files returns an exact primary shared-storage tree and its modes. Persist only the required read mode, use SimpleStorage solely to resolve the root, and route reads through tree-scoped, enumerated IDs and the shared catalog verifier. The independent review required binding reuse to the exact tree and complete generation, delivering cancellation to provider calls, and distinguishing unsupported selection from cancellation; these are separate from detecting an out-of-band physical replacement at the same AOSP document ID. Strict-offline app/data suites passed 264/950 cases, both Android test source sets compiled, and the local signed development pair passed package, manifest permission, OCR payload, alignment, and signer inspection. An earlier lint attempt reached the analyzers but timed out and is not accepted. Detached real Files selection, grant recreation/revocation and the production graph remain separate runtime gates.

The first dedicated app-guest run completed both Android picker-contract tests. The real Files witness reached DocumentsUI but never matched its exact fixture plus affirmative action predicate, and therefore performed no final selection or grant read. The fixture remained byte-identical and the guest's two process exits and allocator release were verified. The next exact witness uses bounded foreground and visible-label diagnostics on the already provisioned guest; no second guest or unsafe retries. The durable locator prerequisite serializes an exact encoded tree URI, an opaque grant ID and returned read/write modes into a private checksummed record. The record is not authority, is not exported, and does not replace the Android persisted-grant check, library marker proof or active-selection publication.

The [folder adapter receipt](../../validation/storage/library-folder-android.md) records the verified timeout recovery and the French DocumentsUI action that stopped the exact first real-folder witness. The next pair admits that observed action without changing locale. The private selection authority stages the exact encoded root locator and verified full generation before pointer replacement. On startup, a folder selection with missing or corrupt supporting records remains reconnect-required, not default. It is a data boundary only until the graph and worker consumers hold the matching activation.

Selection integrity review: a well-formed private generation record with one changed catalog-digest digit reproduced a false reopen. The checksummed private v2 pointer now binds both exact supporting records; the portable marker format is unchanged. A failed support-directory barrier leaves the predecessor selected. The same offline run passed 963 data and 264 app JVM cases and built the exact diagnostic pair; the shared single-original verifier rechecks the catalog and tip before acknowledging streamed bytes, but is not yet the application's external decoder input. Before wiring the live graph, bound retention of unreferenced private locators and exercise cancellation/reconciliation at the pointer boundary. The next runtime checkpoint is the actual result envelope on the retained app guest; do not change grant admission until that evidence is available.

Next core checkpoint: the exact folder wait eliminated an initial navigation race. Real Files returned success, all expected mode flags, and a differently encoded or identified URI; the unchanged adapter refused the tree. Compare its structure with the admitted primary tree in one bounded batch on the same verified guest, without broadening permissions. Checkpoint immediately after that result: correct only a proven platform representation if it is the exact selected fixture, otherwise identify the real provider/navigation mismatch. Separately, the private pointer now has host coverage for missing grant and generation, cancellation before replacement, and lost acknowledgment after replacement. No active graph has been switched.

The structural result identified a canonical local provider tree with a non-`primary` root and one path segment. AOSP API 28's more-specific `home:` Documents namespace explains that shape. Admit exact child trees in that namespace without exchanging URI spellings or accepting the Documents root; the next populated native run must prove the exact fixture URI, a persisted read-only grant, stream hash, recreation and revocation refusal. A private support-store regression also verified that four retained unfinished stages previously failed to prevent another write. Limit new stages and total records in the two private support directories; existing exact records may still reopen and no record is pruned. A future active publication policy must reconcile and bound retained pointers/objects rather than keeping every historical receipt indefinitely.

The [populated API 28 Files witness](../../validation/storage/library-folder-android.md#populated-files-witness) passed all five named tests on the exact checked development pair, including streamed original SHA-256, recreation, revocation and tree-scoped directory descriptors. This is a detached read/descriptor result, not successful write, live selection, or process-death recovery. The independent boundary review requires nonconstructible original membership proof, preflight of aggregate verification bytes before opening any original, and a named populated witness that cannot skip streaming. The immediate successor uses an 8 GiB external verification budget, then retests multi-chunk cancellation/revocation and actual `home:` identity. Next core gate after that batch: project verified records into a private working catalog while retaining originals in the selected folder; then admit only the exact graph selected by the durable pointer. A separate scoped provider-write witness must pass before exposing any folder mutation.

Course check: the first reviewed successor passed host checks (969 data, 264 app, 235 storage) but timed out during the retained guest's first serial lookup, before either installed package was touched. Retry only inside the existing owner-verified boot window; no second guest or reset. The next single app-guest batch combines the exact Files proof and the fresh private Room metadata reopen without copying original files. Reassess at the batch receipt, or within 30 active minutes. Before enabling move, use the observed provider descriptors to qualify physical identity and capacity, then independently verify exclusive creation, final-name publication and paired durability. The ability to sync a read-only directory descriptor is not proof of any of those write conditions.

Detached working-catalog review identified three blocking integration risks: stale rows can escape a callback before the final check, a captured port with an in-flight read can prevent database close without revoking admission, and ordinary cancellation can retain enough closed scratch attempts to block future opens. The corrections must fence each read against the exact current tree, revoke admission before teardown, and count spill and working catalog in the same attempt; retain only scratch whose closure or cleanup remains uncertain. No user-facing folder action is enabled by the detached metadata graph.

The reviewed working-catalog successor now has 971 data and 264 app JVM cases, 236 storage host tests and both locally verified development packages. Next evidence is the single combined API 28 batch on the retained app guest. Future production composition must make an activation, not a bare page/job ID, the identity at Activity, picker callback, editor, export and WorkManager boundaries. Keep legacy unscoped WorkManager wakeups inert once activation-scoped enrollment is enabled; rediscover their actual still-pending Room jobs from the selected graph instead of repointing old wakes. A detached catalog or staged metadata return is not the active graph and cannot authorize a user-visible switch.

The [reviewed combined Files and Room witness](../../validation/storage/library-folder-android.md#populated-files-witness) passed 6/6 real API 28 instrumentation cases, including a `home:` grant, mid-stream cancellation and revocation, and a staged Room reopen with no duplicate originals. Four canceled callbacks did not exhaust the private scratch budget. The dedicated app guest is stopped and released. Next core slice: connect exact verified external selection to the private durable pointer and a graph opened only for the matching activation; fail closed to **Reconnect library** while retaining the old selection. Device provider writes, Settings actions, retirement, complete portable masks/Undo and known historical-conversion rendering remain open; no merge or publication.

First-use activation starts by assigning the existing default store a persistent opaque identity without moving originals or changing Room. Publish a complete initial pointer inside a single private settings-directory move. Only positively absent settings may bootstrap; a missing pointer under an existing settings directory is an integrity failure. Once the settings root might be visible, reopen and acknowledge that exact identity instead of choosing another one. Follow with a real active graph, not a second bootstrap or a Settings placeholder.

Three first-use regressions passed in 974 data JVM cases: stable default identity, missing pointer under an existing root, and uncertain final directory sync. The ordinary application must now obtain that identity before opening or reconciling the default Room graph. This startup guard is not live switching: a selected folder is still structurally refused until its graph and **Reconnect library** interaction exist. The exact next API 28 batch must show the current default graph holds the durable identity across Activity recreation without modifying any unrelated guest state.

The first activation pair passed 7/7 API 28 cases, including the durable default identity across Activity recreation. Independent review found that ordinary compose test fixtures with a lazily uncreated files directory would break, definite early failures could exhaust the first-use stage quota, and acknowledging first use needed a barrier on the parent of the app files directory. The two host regressions reproduced these latter issues. Correct only attempt-owned stages before a final rename can start; leave a possibly published root for reconciliation. The next bounded batch exercises cold service composition over existing rows and originals, fail-closed startup without creating default Room, and affected recovery, routing, editor and renderer paths. In that same app-guest batch, an opt-in write probe touches one new child of a fresh synthetic tree only. Neither successful startup nor one provider operation permits selected-folder writes, source retirement, or publication. Reassess at the receipt or within 30 active minutes.

The [activation compatibility and provider test](../../validation/storage/library-folder-android.md#startup-compatibility-and-write-qualification) passed 16/17: all ordinary-store and detached cases passed, and API 28 `renameDocument` reported a missing old path after staging and closed readback. Treat its result as uncertain, retain the probe files, and never replay that rename. The next exact synthetic check uses `moveDocument` from an isolated staging child with the final basename, verifies both parents and tests destination collision. In parallel with read-only design work, keep production mutation disabled and make the next active-folder consumer open the exact committed generation, not just the latest visible tip.

The separate staged-move probe passed 8/8 API 28 cases without changing any original fixture leaf; the guest is stopped and released. A single cooperative move and observed collision refusal are not external publication acceptance. Main lane input `28f1392`: reopen a privately selected exact folder through its recorded generation and activation, with a scoped working Room catalog and no original duplication. Refuse a whole mismatching generation or a revoked grant while preserving the pointer. A durable pointer is not yet an editable graph, app Settings, a journal or source-retirement authority; return to the real app navigation after proving the startup consumer. Reassess at native evidence or within 30 active minutes.

The first recorded-folder candidate `a2f81cd` passed all seven named API 28 tests. Independent review found four Important gaps: selected-only startup could bootstrap default settings when none existed; a late revocation could escape as raw I/O; a scope could revive after observing a changed activation; and interrupted Room population could exhaust retained scratch even after a successful close. The follow-up tests the first-use read and latching gate, maps known provider loss separately from local I/O and callback failure, and runs preverification and Room staging in the same bounded private attempt. The native successor must preserve the selection on both entry and post-read revocation, retire closed scratch, and refuse a revived stale scope. The exact stage-population cancellation and close-failure injection witnesses remain separate coverage. No ordinary app folder activation or write permission is enabled by this slice.

Next implementation order: first use the witnessed same-basename provider move behind the existing object-transfer and catalog-publication ports, with physical root checks, capacity and exact private intent before any managed payload. Qualify this by exporting a populated default library to a fresh synthetic Files folder and reopening its unchanged original and saved rows; this leaves the active library untouched and exercises a real folder publication rather than a test-only write. In the active graph, original reads must lend a seekable file only for one verified decode. Current `CommittedSources.load` hands out an unscoped `File`, and the original-preview and export consumers have separate metadata lookups; do not route external reads by returning a disposable path from that API. Keep metadata binding separate from the byte-file loan, and use the same exact bound source throughout decoding and rendering. Before enabling any live switch, route foreground continuations and WorkManager wakes through the issuing activation.

The 7/7 API 28 selected-catalog follow-up passed on `693b92d` with the late-revoke and stale-scope guards. New publication-stage tests are intentionally ahead of their implementation: no repeat mutation after a lost response, no adoption of a byte-identical unrelated file, no acknowledgment before both directory barriers, and no move from recovery-only admission. The first host invocation stopped before compilation on a generated Gradle transform with missing output. Preserve it; replay strictly offline in an isolated Gradle user home containing the existing pinned module and wrapper bytes. No production writer or new device action starts from the failed invocation.

The isolated replay reached the intended missing-publication-type compilation failure. The host successor passed 983 data JVM cases, including five named low-level publication cases, and compiled the data Android tests. The five use actual same-volume file moves and a hard-link identity oracle; responses, durability faults and late replacement are injected. The Android stage/destination descriptor adapter and its durable recovery intent have not yet consumed these checks. Keep independent boundary review open and count no new provider or end-to-end acceptance from the host result.

Publication review requires cancellation checks between separate provider lookups, durable consumption of the one-shot move authorization even if the call leaves no visible change, and a precise lifetime for the recorded file identity. `st_dev` and `st_ino` are acceptable only with an independently bound live descriptor and stable parents; never infer crash-safe identity from an inode number and matching digest alone. An uncertain leaf cannot pass the ordinary existing-object or catalog-reuse paths on bytes alone. Recover with an exact qualified witness or retain both locations and report the uncertainty. The next actual Android gate is an adapter witness with the same live descriptor retained across the staged move and both final readbacks.

The host review follow-up passed 986 data JVM cases (eight named moves), 264 app JVM cases and data Android compilation. The separate core, fixture and image suites recorded 323, 15 and 283 cases respectively. The one-shot store hook is now mandatory; the test exercises incomplete authorization with an injected in-memory journal, not durable Android recording. The provider adapter must implement the durable hook and compare live readback against the same still-open descriptor before these host checks can be credited to export. The single-leaf helper deletes no file.

For the exact writable permission boundary, a read-only picker handoff must never promote itself. Only an explicit writable-destination command may persist missing read/write bits from the actual returned tree, and only if that result included write. The focused contract reproduced at compilation. The Android witness calls the explicit handoff for the fresh synthetic provider child and releases only the write mode it acquired. An already authorized alias must not substitute for the selected URI.

Provider write next: bind the exact persisted tree to retained `fstat` directory observations, check available bytes before any managed payload, and initially admit only a newly allocated backup child under the picked destination. Two different folder URIs or two different inode numbers are not proof that mutable trees are disjoint; keep selected-root append and retirement disabled until physical alias and ancestry checks exist. Persist the exact one-shot publication intent before a move; verification of an uncertain leaf cannot substitute a newly opened file with matching bytes when its identity anchor is lost. Use this boundary through `LibraryObjectTransfer` and `LibraryCatalogPublication`, not a second catalog engine. The first integration witness is normal default-store ingest and edit → external export → selected folder readback, with the original bytes and accepted recipe unchanged and no source cleanup. Recheck at the actual Files grant result and the first descriptor-adapter proof or within 30 active minutes.

The explicit write-grant handoff passed 9/9 API 28 instrumentation cases on `389bb31`; the read-only open still acquires no write. The capacity follow-up checks the trusted local provider, exact persisted modes, and simultaneous directory identity before delivering a checked free-byte count. Review requires a single fail-fast provider lane through descriptor close, not just a cancellation signal. The one-shot spend record adds a separate private operation gate: exclusive creation, forced contents and directory acknowledgment before permission to invoke the move, with fail-closed readback of any uncertain record. It is neither the whole export intent nor a guarantee of moved file identity after process death.

Boundary follow-up: 9/9 API 28 cases on `852d6d2` passed the exact-capacity grant, alias and mode-loss controls; existing fixture bytes and the released guest are accounted for in the [receipt](../../validation/storage/library-folder-android.md). The shared provider lane and private one-shot spend passed strict offline with 991 data and 265 app JVM cases and both Android test compilations. Host cases cover an uncooperative canceled call holding the lane, fresh-instance duplicate and remapped-parent refusal, invalid/trailing spend data retention and injected barriers before and after record creation. The injected host barrier is not Windows or Android durability evidence. The native replay for the shared lane will be grouped with the first real backup, rather than consuming a separate guest slot.

Course check, same coordinator and task: the main lane is ordinary default-library import and accepted edit → actual Files grant → newly named backup → independent reopen, with no source cleanup or active change. Gate: hold exact source/destination capabilities, bound capacity before writes, make a private durable intent before creating the backup child, and bind staged/final names to live file and directory identities before spending a move. Core: reuse the saved-catalog writer, object transfer, catalog publisher and exact original readback. Settings and active selected-folder append wait for this evidence; broader provider support, performance claims and unrelated acquisition remain outside the current slot. One serial offline build slot and one bounded already authorized app-guest batch; recheck at the compile RED or first provider result, and no later than 30 active minutes.

`LIBRARY-PORTABILITY-01` default-backup attempt from `fe914fd`: the ordinary-store probe reproduced the missing export entry at both test call sites. Its source uses isolated ordinary ApplicationServices ingestion and an accepted rotation; the destination is one new child of the exact Files fixture grant. The successor closes and validates one fresh Room stage, reserves only the distinct payload bytes and staging space, records the operation privately, and runs the shared object and catalog publishers. The marker remains last; an interrupted attempt is not automatically reopened for writing. The external provider leaf and parent descriptors now have to prove the same file around both directory barriers, rather than trusting the move result. Independent boundary review and the first actual native export remain outstanding. The first GREEN compilation exposed a private page-limit visibility issue; the next build reached Android test compilation and the known Kotlin temporary-backup failure (INC-008). No APK was installed from either failed invocation.

The independent export review found four Important issues. The candidate now compares authoritative source and blob fields separately from rebuilt counters, opens the stage nontruncating and only writes to the empty anchored inode, checks each directory against held identities before descending, and verifies exact completed payload namespaces before writing the marker. Export rejects backup graphs above the same unique-original byte cap as the existing reader. Exact privately forced intent and completion records remain if delivery is canceled or uncertain, rather than being deleted before the caller receives success. Scope limit: the test-only entry has a bounded retained-attempt quota; before Settings exposure, bind confirmation to a stable operation/issuing activation, and track acknowledged results without replay. The native witness independently checks closed bytes and metadata through the exact provider grant; a second actual read-only Files selection of the backup child and interruption/recovery controls remain separate gates.

Course check: the [real default-library Files export](../../validation/storage/library-folder-android.md#default-library-backup-candidate) passed 10/10 on API 28. The next core witness selects the actual new backup child independently, persists only its exact read grant, reopens its closed catalog and lends a single seekable original through native decode; the default pointer and original must still be unchanged. Use only attempt-owned bounded scratch and refuse results after lost access or a changed generation. The loan is not the editable selected application graph and does not authorize switching, worker enrollment or source cleanup. Keep the four-attempt export quota and unacknowledged-receipt UI work open rather than adding a second export action. Recheck at the first combined picker/decode witness or within 30 active minutes.

The independent child-reopen test reproduced the missing single-original loan at compilation. The loan retains at most one current scratch original across its structured native consumer, authenticates the actual closed private bytes, and rechecks the granted catalog before acknowledging the decode. Ordinary cancellation discards a definitely closed attempt; uncertain closure or cleanup is bounded and never touches the source. The app test uses another actual Files result for the child rather than inventing a derived grant from its parent. Current renderer and preview callers still obtain unscoped private files; no selection can use this callback to publish a decoder result, change the graph or enroll a background job without its issuing activation.

The next application graph increment should pair the selected catalog and original reader under one recorded activation before changing UI destinations. The default import, preview, thumbnail, editor and OCR consumers currently resolve their store independently; each continuation must either borrow that issuing activation through its operation or fail before opening a different graph. Background transport must include the activation in both payload and uniqueness identity, and rediscover pending Room jobs instead of retargeting legacy wakes. A catalog that can browse or decode without a ready selected-folder writer must not acknowledge saved edits; an editable graph still needs its verified append path. These are dependencies of the user journey, not acceptance from the detached loan.

The original-loan review required an explicit read-only, non-escaping callback and a recoverable distinction between failure to open an empty attempt and failure to close an already written copy. The focused scratch test reached the missing-helper compile refusal. The extracted scratch owner removes only the empty attempt on open failure and retains any visible unacknowledged leaf; the ordinary close and decode path removes only its own known copy. The actual Files fixture now cancels a suspended coroutine, revokes the child grant after a successful decode, checks empty scratch by successful enumeration, and rereads the original and pointer after the child journey. Authenticated generation drift after native decode and cross-build evidence remain unrun.

The [combined export, fresh Files reopen and native decode](../../validation/storage/library-folder-android.md#default-library-backup-candidate) passed 10/10 on `1e801c4`; all temporary originals were retired in the tested success, cancel and revoke paths. The app guest was stopped and released. Next gate: before a switch can enter the real app graph, replace unscoped OCR WorkManager wakeups with activation-bound deliveries. Verify they cannot open a different library, even if it has the same page, job or library identity at a different activation. The source of truth for migration is existing pending Room work discovered again from the current activation. Also bind queued foreground save notifications to their issuing graph before switching the library; no Settings or activation write follows from a transport-only pass. One offline build slot; recheck at the first delivery checks or within 30 active minutes.

`LIBRARY-PORTABILITY-01` activation-scoped OCR attempt, input `13e89f0`: one four-field WorkManager envelope and uniqueness key, exact recorded default activation before opening the worker graph, and activation-bound save notifications from ordinary import, capture, editor, batch Done and Undo. Scope: app OCR scheduling and its current composition/call sites, the read-only default activation lookup, affected JVM/Android tests, and the exact-pair runner and contract records. The missing transport and activation-specific reconciliation reproduced at Kotlin compilation. Existing queued two-field wakes are inert; foreground rediscovers pending Room jobs rather than translating old WorkManager requests. The runtime check must prove that legacy and mismatched activation wakes do not start a real pending job even with identical library/page/job IDs, that ordinary startup finds that same job, and that ordinary saves and accepted edits still produce searchable OCR. Only the default graph participates. Ruling: the precheck protects the current default-only graph but is not a switchable lifetime lease; keep the graph switch and old root retirement disabled. One serial offline build and one bounded app-guest batch; recheck on the native receipt or within 30 active minutes.

The offline candidate recorded 1,885 JVM cases across the five modules (271 app and 993 data) without failure or skip; 237 storage checks passed. The first extended build stopped at the rediscovery fixture's Kotlin `Unit` return inference after unit tests, with no install. The corrected fixture checks WorkManager success independently of the one Room recognition start; its app and data test sources compile, and the exact development app/test packages passed the local APK checks. The new native batch uses the retained app-test guest only. Raw transcripts and packages remain local.

Independent review found no Critical production issue within the default-only scope. Important witness corrections: observe the uninitialized retained graph on a cold launch with no Activity; compare the current job, generation, input digest and producing job independently in Room; wait for foreground scan completion before exercising the genuine save and accepted-edit notifications and inspect their exact WorkManager keys; and exercise batch Done after deselection followed by Undo. The pending-tile test still has no adversarial same-target/different-activation overlay observation. Ruling: candidate-side rediscovery checks legacy payload behavior and exact job retention, not a real APK-upgrade migration of a queued WorkSpec.
